STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY — preserve these established static environment facts:
The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.
These facts constrain static architecture, fixed fixtures, persistent furniture, entrances, surfaces, and lighting sources only. The dragon licks its lips, then takes a slow sip, letting the liquid swirl across its tongue. Amy watches as the creature exhales contentedly; she wipes the bar with a fresh cloth, smoothing out any drips of ale that have fallen onto the wood.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.

RAW SCENE
Amy lifts her cloth from the polished tabletop and sets it aside beside the counter.  
She steps toward the barrel by the hearth on the west wall, grabs its lid with one hand, and tilts the barrel to pour liquid into a chipped mug held in front of her.  
The green‑skinned goblin leans over the east‑side counter, clutching the mug with his right hand while his left hand rests on the polished tabletop for support.  
Liquid cascades from the barrel into the mug, filling it as Amy pours. The goblin’s face lights up and he nods in thanks at the refill.

Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.

RAW SCENE
The goblin’s mug fills with a steady cascade from the barrel beside the hearth; his face lights up and he nods in thanks as Amy pours.

An elf with silver hair streaming down her shoulders pushes open the south door of the Stone Hearth Tavern and steps into the hall, walking across the ten‑foot wide aisle toward the back table in the northwest corner. She strides slowly, taking a graceful half‑step at each footfall so that her walk from entrance to table takes about two seconds.

She reaches the back table’s wooden surface, sits down with a soft thud of her chair against the floorboards, and looks up as Amy approaches from the counter.

Amy lifts a crystal chalice into hand, then pours a steaming cup of ale into it. The liquid bubbles over the rim in a pale amber glow. She sets the chalice gently on the back table’s wooden top, aligning it with the carved edge of the wood. With her head tilted slightly she nods toward the elf as a quiet thanks before turning to take another sip from the crystal cup.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.

RAW SCENE
The goblin’s mug fills with a steady cascade from the barrel beside the hearth; his face lights up and he nods in thanks as Amy pours.

An elf with silver hair streaming down her shoulders pushes open the south door of the Stone Hearth Tavern and steps into the hall, walking across the ten‑foot wide aisle toward the back table in the northwest corner. She strides slowly, taking a graceful half‑step at each footfall so that her walk from entrance to table takes about two seconds.

She reaches the back table’s wooden surface, pulls out a polished oak chair and settles into it with a soft thud against the floorboards, and looks up as Amy approaches from the counter.

Amy lifts a crystal chalice into hand, then pours a steaming cup of ale into it. The liquid bubbles over the rim in a pale amber glow. She sets the chalice gently on the back table’s wooden top, aligning it with the carved edge of the wood. With her head tilted slightly she nods toward the elf as a quiet thanks before turning to take another sip from the crystal cup.

She strides slowly, taking a graceful half‑step at each footfall so that her walk from entrance to table takes about two seconds.

She reaches the back table’s wooden surface, pulls out a polished oak chair and settles into it with a soft thud against the floorboards, and looks up as Amy approaches from the counter.

Amy lifts a crystal chalice in her hand, then pours a steaming cup of ale into it. With a gentle slide she places the chalice onto the back table’s wooden top, transferring it from her palm to the surface and aligning it with the carved edge of the wood. She tilts her head slightly and nods toward the elf as a quiet thanks before turning to take another sip from the crystal cup.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.

RAW SCENE
The goblin’s mug fills with a steady cascade from the barrel beside the hearth; his face lights up and he nods in thanks as Amy pours.

An elf with silver hair streaming down her shoulders pushes open the south door of the Stone Hearth Tavern and steps into the hall, walking across the ten‑foot wide aisle toward the back table in the northwest corner. She strides slowly, taking a graceful half‑step at each footfall so that her walk from entrance to table takes about two seconds.

She reaches the back table’s wooden surface, pulls out a polished oak chair and settles into it with a soft thud against the floorboards, and looks up as Amy approaches from the counter.

Amy lifts a crystal chalice in her hand, then pours a steaming cup of ale into it. She gently slides the chalice from her palm and sets it on the back table’s wooden top beside the carved edge. With a gentle slide she places the chalice onto the back table’s wooden top, transferring it from her palm to the surface and aligning it with the carved edge of the wood. She tilts her head slightly and nods toward the elf as a quiet thanks before turning to take another sip from the crystal cup.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.

RAW SCENE
The goblin’s mug fills with a steady cascade from the barrel beside the hearth; his face lights up and he nods in thanks as Amy pours.

An elf with silver hair streaming down her shoulders pushes open the south door of the Stone Hearth Tavern and steps into the hall, walking across the ten‑foot wide aisle toward the back table in the northwest corner. She strides slowly, taking a graceful half‑step at each footfall so that her walk from entrance to table takes about two seconds.

She reaches the back table’s wooden surface, sits in an oak chair already there and settles into it with a soft thud against the floorboards, and looks up as Amy approaches from the counter.

Amy lifts a crystal chalice in her hand, then pours a steaming cup of ale into it. She gently slides the chalice from her palm and sets it on the back table’s wooden top beside the carved edge. With a gentle slide she places the chalice onto the back table’s wooden top, transferring it from her palm to the surface and aligning it with the carved edge of the wood. She tilts her head slightly and nods toward the elf as a quiet thanks before turning to take another sip from the crystal cup.
